## P15 15 - Search Intent and Keyword Research

**原文**：

Search intent is the reason behind a query. In traditional SEO, we often begin with a keyword list, volume, difficulty, and ranking opportunity. In AI SEO, that is still useful, but it is not enough. AI answer engines try to understand the job the user wants done, the context around that job, the stage of the journey. and the format of answer that will help. That is why the slide separates the query from the intent signals. The words in the query are only the surface. Underneath the user may need education, comparison, pricing, troubleshooting or step-by-step action. When your content matches that deeper intent, it becomes easier for search engines and AI. systems to select it as a useful answer. The key takeaway is simple. Keyword describe what people type, but intent explains why they are asking. A practical AI SEO workflow starts with a C term, but it should not end there. First choose the broad topic you want to be discovered for. Then cluster that topic by intent. so you are not mixing how-to questions, comparison questions, and buying questions into the same content plan. After that, expand each cluster into realistic AI prompts. These are not just short keywords. They are complete questions, a buyer learner or customer might ask chatGPT Gemini perplexity or Google AI. The next step is comparison. Look at both classic search results and AI answers, because they reveal different source patterns and content gaps. Finally, turn the research into a content brief. The validation lane keeps the workflow grounded. Use search volume to understand demand, answer gaps to find opportunity and citation patterns to learn. what types of sources are being trusted. Intent categories help organize keyboard research into useful decisions. Information and intent usually signals that the user wants to learn something, such as how a concept works or what a term means. Commercial intent means the user is evaluating options, alternatives, vendors, features or trade-offs. Transactional intent is closer to action, pricing, demos, trials, purchase pages, templates, or sign-up flows. Support intent is often ignored in SEO planning, but it matters a lot in AI search because users ask AI tools how to fix configure or troubleshoot products. In reality, AI platforms often blend. these intents into one answer. A single response might explain a concept, compare tools, recommend next steps and site sources. That means your research should not force every query into one rigid box. Use the intent type as a starting point, then decide what evidence, format, and action the answer needs. Keyboard research becomes more powerful when it turns into prompt research. The top table represents the familiar SEO view, seed keywords, estimated demand, and ranking difficulty. That helps you understand the market, but it does not fully capture how people ask AI systems for help. The lower table expands the same topic into realistic prompts each map to intent. and source need. A recommendation prompt needs comparisons and trade-offs. A how-to prompt needs steps and best practices. A cost prompt needs pricing, clarity, assumptions, and limits. This is where AI SEO becomes more precise. Instead of creating one generic page for a broad keyword, you build a map of the exact... questions by us ask and the evidence each answer requires. The file structure on the right reinforces the output, seed terms, prompt maps, and content briefs become reusable assets for your SEO workflow. In a real team workflow, intent research needs ownership and repeatability. Start by researching both queries and prompts. So your plan includes traditional search demand and AI style questions. Then compare what appears in Google results with what AI platforms produce. The goal is not to copy the answer. The goal is to understand which sources are visible, what facts are being reused and where your content is missing. Prioritization comes next. Google's on-gap. that align with business value, not just high volume. A low volume prompt can still matter if it appears late in the buyer journey or influences a decision. When the team publishes, the content should be answered ready. Clear structure, direct answers, supporting evidence and a format that can be understood by both humans and machines. The governance room... matters because someone should own each brief, verify evidence, and refresh the research when results change. Feel for intent, not just rank.

**译文**：

搜索意图是用户发起查询背后的原因。在传统 SEO 中，我们通常从关键词列表、搜索量、难度和排名机会开始。在 AI SEO 中，这些仍然有用，但还不够。AI 答案引擎试图理解用户想要完成的任务、该任务相关的上下文、用户所处的旅程阶段，以及哪种答案格式能提供帮助。这就是为什么这张幻灯片将查询与意图信号区分开来。查询中的词语只是表面现象。在表面之下，用户可能需要了解信息、进行比较、获取报价、解决问题或获得分步操作指导。当你的内容与这种更深层的意图相匹配时，搜索引擎和 AI 系统就更容易将其选为有用的答案。关键结论很简单：关键词描述的是人们输入的内容，而意图解释的是他们提问的原因。一个实用的 AI SEO 工作流程始于一个核心词，但不应止于此。首先选择你希望被发现的广泛主题。然后按意图对该主题进行分组，这样你就不会将操作指南类问题、比较类问题和购买类问题混入同一个内容计划中。之后，将每个分组扩展成真实的 AI 提示。这些不仅仅是简短的关键词。 这些都是买家、学习者或客户可能会向 ChatGPT、Gemini、Perplexity 或 Google AI 提出的完整问题。下一步是进行比较。既要查看经典的搜索结果，也要分析 AI 的回答，因为它们揭示了不同的来源模式和内容空白。最后，将研究成果转化为内容简报。验证环节能确保工作流程不偏离实际。利用搜索量了解需求，通过回答空白发现机会，并借助引用模式来学习哪些类型的来源更受信任。意图分类有助于将关键词研究整理为有用的决策依据。信息类意图通常表明用户希望了解某些内容，例如某个概念如何运作或某个术语的含义。商业意图意味着用户正在评估各种选项、替代方案、供应商、功能或权衡因素。交易意图则更接近具体行动，例如价格、演示、试用、购买页面、模板或注册流程。支持意图在 SEO 规划中常被忽视，但在 AI 搜索中却非常重要，因为用户会向 AI 工具咨询如何修复、配置或排查产品问题。实际上，AI 平台常常将这些意图融合在同一个答案中。 单次回应可能涵盖概念解释、工具对比、后续步骤推荐以及资源来源。这意味着你的研究不应将每个查询都强行套入单一的固定框架。应以意图类型为起点，再决定答案需要哪些证据、采用何种格式以及采取何种行动。当键盘搜索转化为提示词研究时，其效果会显著增强。上表呈现了熟悉的 SEO 视角：种子关键词、预估需求量和排名难度。这有助于你了解市场，但无法完全体现人们如何向 AI 系统寻求帮助。下表则将同一主题扩展为多个贴近实际的提示词，每个提示词都对应相应的意图类型和资源需求。推荐类提示词需要对比分析与权衡取舍；操作指南类提示词需要分步说明和最佳实践；成本类提示词则需要价格信息、清晰说明、假设前提及限制条件。这正是 AI SEO 实现精准化的关键所在。你无需为宽泛关键词制作单一的通用页面，而是构建一份映射用户实际问题的完整图谱，并明确每个答案所需的依据。右侧的文件结构进一步巩固了这一输出成果：种子词、提示词图谱和内容简报均可作为可复用的 SEO 工作流程资产。 在真实的团队工作流程中，意图研究需要具备归属感和可重复性。首先从研究查询和提示两方面入手。因此，你的计划应包括传统的搜索需求以及 AI 风格的问题。然后，将 Google 搜索结果与 AI 平台生成的内容进行对比。目标不是复制答案，而是了解哪些来源可见、哪些事实被重复使用，以及你的内容在哪些方面有所缺失。接下来是优先级排序。Google 的“未满足需求”需要与业务价值对齐，而不仅仅是高流量。即使某个低流量提示出现在买家旅程的后期阶段或能影响决策，它仍然具有重要价值。当团队发布内容时，应确保内容已准备好回答问题：结构清晰、答案直接、有支撑证据，并采用人类和机器都能理解的格式。治理环节至关重要——应为每个简报指定负责人，核实证据，并在结果变化时更新研究内容。要感知意图，而不仅仅是追求排名。

## P16 16 - Crawlability, Indexability and Canonicalization

**原文**：

When we talk about technical SEO, it is easy to jump straight to keywords, content quality or backlinks. But none of that matters if the right pages cannot be reached, evaluated and consolidated correctly. Crawlability answers the first question. Can a search bot, discover and fetch the page through links, site maps and robots through. Indexibility answers the second question. After the page is fetched, is it eligible to appear in the index or is it blocked by no index errors, redirects or duplicated content? Canonicalization answers the third question. When several URLs look similar, which one should receive the ranking and retrieval signals? In AI SEO, these foundations are even more important because answer engines need clean, reliable source candidates. A page that is blocked, excluded or split across duplicates is less likely to become a trusted retrieval source. Think of the process as a pipeline. First, the system needs the way to discover the URL, usually through internal links and XML site map. Next, the bot checks robots.txt to decide whether crawling is allowed. If access is allowed, the page must be fetched and rendered well enough for the important HTML, links and content to be understood. After that, indexability signals decide whether the page is eligible, status code, no index tags, redirects. and other blocking conditions all matter. Then canonical selection tells the system whether this URL is the preferred page or whether its signals should be consolidated elsewhere. Only after those earlier steps are clean, does the page become a better candidate for retrieval in search results and AI answers. In the order matters, you cannot fix canonical First, if the bot cannot access the page at all. The audit becomes much easier when you separate the signals into three groups. For crawlability, check robots.txt, XML site maps, internal links, and HTTP 200 responses. These signals tell you whether bots can discover and retrieve the page. For index. This ability look for no indexed directives, redirect behavior, blocked resources and fin on mere duplicate pages. These signals decide whether the page is eligible to be stored and served. For canonicalization, Inspector L equals sign canonical tags, parameter URLs, HTTP versions, HTTPS versions, and trailings. these are the places where duplicate or alternate URLs can split authority. The important point is not to memorize a random checklist. The goal is to understand what each signal controls. Crawlability controls access, indexability controls, eligibility and canonicalization controls consolidation to the preferred URL. In an enterprise environment, these checks should not depend only on a manual audit after traffic drops. The safer pattern is to build SEO validation into the delivery workflow. A developer may change a template, route, metadata rule, side-match generator, or redirect configuration. Before that change reaches production, continue. Newer integration can run automated checks for robots, rules, cyber-map validity, canonical consistency, redirect chains, and indexable page status. SEO QA can then review edge cases, important templates and intentional exceptions. After deployment, monitoring tools and search console data help detect regressions that slip through. Notice the feedback loop, production findings should flow back into the backlog and into stronger validation rules. The takeaway is practical, treat crawlability, indexability, and canonicalization as release quality concerns, not as one time SEO clean up tasks. A useful way to end every audit is to follow the paths to the preferred. page. Start with access. Can bots reach this URL and our robots rules or server errors blocking them. Then move to eligibility. Can the page be indexed or is a no index directive redirect or invalid response preventing it? Next, check consolidation. Is this URL self canonical or does it correctly point to an error? another preferred version. After that, confirm discovery and freshness through the site map and internal links. Finally, compare duplicate or parameter variants to make sure value is not being split across competing URLs. The bottom line is simple. Fix access first eligibility second and consolidation third. That sequence keeps audit grounded and prevents teams from spending time polishing a page that search systems cannot crawl, index or trust.

**译文**：

当我们谈论技术性 SEO 时，很容易直接想到关键词、内容质量或反向链接。但如果无法正确访问、评估和整合相关页面，这些都毫无意义。可爬取性（Crawlability）回答了第一个问题：搜索引擎机器人能否通过链接、站点地图和 robots.txt 文件发现并抓取页面？可索引性（Indexibility）回答了第二个问题：页面被抓取后，是否有资格进入索引，还是会因为 noindex 错误、重定向或重复内容而被屏蔽？规范化（Canonicalization）则回答了第三个问题：当多个 URL 看起来相似时，哪一个应该获得排名和抓取信号？在 AI SEO 中，这些基础更为重要，因为答案引擎需要干净、可靠的来源候选页面。一个被屏蔽、被排除或分散在多个重复页面中的网页，不太可能成为可信的检索来源。可以把这个过程看作一个流程管道：首先，系统需要找到访问 URL 的途径，通常通过内部链接和 XML 站点地图；接着，机器人会检查 robots.txt 文件，以确定是否允许抓取。 如果允许访问，页面必须被获取并充分渲染，以便重要的 HTML、链接和内容能够被理解。之后，可索引性信号会决定页面是否符合资格，状态码、no index 标签、重定向以及其他阻塞条件都会产生影响。接着，规范选择会告知系统该 URL 是否为首选页面，或其信号应被合并到其他位置。只有在这些早期步骤都通过后，页面才更有可能在搜索结果和 AI 答案中被检索。顺序很重要，如果爬虫根本无法访问页面，就无法优先解决规范问题。当将这些信号分为三组时，审计会变得更加容易。对于可爬行性，请检查 robots.txt、XML 站点地图、内部链接以及 HTTP 200 响应。这些信号表明爬虫是否能够发现并获取页面。对于可索引性，请检查 noindex 指令、重定向行为、被阻塞的资源以及重复页面。这些信号决定页面是否有资格被存储和提供。 对于规范化处理，Inspector L 会检查等号规范标签、参数 URL、HTTP 版本、HTTPS 版本以及末尾斜杠。这些位置都可能导致重复或备用 URL 分裂网站权限。关键在于不要死记硬背一份随机的检查清单，而是要理解每个信号分别控制什么。可抓取性控制访问权限，可索引性控制资格，而规范化处理则控制向首选 URL 的合并。在企业环境中，这些检查不应仅仅依赖流量下降后的手动审计。更安全的方式是将 SEO 验证嵌入到交付流程中。开发人员可能会更改模板、路由、元数据规则、侧边匹配生成器或重定向配置。在该更改上线生产之前，新的集成流程可以自动运行检查，验证 robots 文件、规则、站点地图有效性、规范一致性、重定向链以及页面的可索引状态。随后，SEO 质量保证团队可以审查边缘情况、重要模板以及有意的例外情况。部署后，监控工具和搜索控制台数据有助于检测漏网的回归问题。 注意反馈循环：生产环境的发现应回流至待办事项列表，并转化为更严格的验证规则。关键在于实践层面：应将可爬取性、可索引性和规范化处理视为发布质量关切，而非一次性 SEO 清理任务。每次审计结束时，一个有效的方法是追踪通往首选页面的路径。首先从访问性开始：机器人能否访问此 URL？我们的 robots 规则或服务器错误是否阻止了它们？接着是资格性：该页面能否被索引？是否存在 noindex 指令、重定向或无效响应阻止了索引？接下来检查合并性：此 URL 是否自我规范，或是否正确指向另一个首选版本（而非错误页面）？之后，通过站点地图和内部链接确认页面的可发现性和时效性。最后，比较重复页面或参数变体，确保价值不会因多个竞争 URL 而被分散。核心原则很简单：先解决访问性问题，再处理资格性问题，最后处理合并性问题。这一顺序能确保审计工作务实落地，避免团队将时间浪费在打磨那些搜索引擎无法爬取、索引或信任的页面上。

## P17 17 - On-Page SEO and Content Quality

**原文**：

On page SU is no longer just about placing a keyword in a title and hoping the page ranks. In an AI search environment, the page has to be easy for crawlers, retrievers, and answer engines to understand. The center of the visual is a well structured article, because that is where the optimization actually lives. On the left, we start with... 3 inputs, clear intent, useful structure and trusted evidence. These are the foundations that help a page satisfy a real user need. On the right, the same page is interpreted by search results and AI answer systems. The key idea is simple. When a page is organized around user intent backed by evidence and written clearly, becomes easier to crawl, easier to sight and easier to summarize. For this lecture, think of on-page SEO as the bridge between human usefulness and machine readability. A strong page usually follows a repeatable quality flow, it begins with search intent because the page must match what the searcher is trying to accomplish. From there. The content brief turns that intent into a plan, audience, key questions, angle, sources, and success metrics. Next comes page structure. Tierheadings, logical sections, and scanable formatting help both readers and retrieval systems understand the page. The 4th step adds evidence and entities. gives the page credibility while entities help search systems connect the content to known topics, people, products, places, and attributes. Finally, the page is published with the quality checklist. The outcome is not just a page that can rank, it is a page that can be cited in an AI answer, included in a summary or selected as a useful source. The practical takeaway is to design pages as structured answers, not just keyword targets. Content quality is built from several signals working together. Original insight matters because AI search systems and traditional search engines both need content that adds value beyond repeating what is already available. Topical coverage helps the page answer the main question and related follow-up questions. Entity clarity is important because clear names, concepts, products and attributes make the page easier to connect to a knowledge graph or retrieval index. Freshness matters when the topic changes over time, especially for tools, pricing, policies, and industry practices. author trust strengthens confidence when the page explains who is behind the information and why they are qualified. Redouble user experience keeps the page accessible, short sections, helpful headings and team formatting, none of these signals works alone. Together they create a page that feels helpful to a human and understandable to search engines and E.I.A. and systems. Now, let's translate quality into specific on-page elements. The title tag sets the promise of the page in search results, the H1 confirms the main topic once the reader arrives. H2 sections organize the page into meaningful sub topics, which makes the content easier to scan and easier to retrieve in chunks. Internal link. connect the page to related pages, helping both users and crawlers understand the broader topic cluster. schema markup adds structured data that can clarify the page type, author, date, FAQ content, and other important details. FAQ blocks are useful when the answer real questions instead of adding filler. The file list on the right reminds that on-page quality is supported by technical files and structured formats, including marked-down content, schema data, robots.txt, and sitemap.xml. The practical lesson is that content and structure should work together. A useful article becomes stronger when it's meaning is clearly encoded on the page. In a real... team on page SEO should be treated as a quality review workflow, not a last minute checklist. The draft begins with research, a useful outline, sources and an internal review. Then the SEO review checks intent alignment, keyword coverage, titles, meta information and internal links. The evidence check protects the page from weak. claims by reviewing credibility, fact accuracy, data freshness and quote or link quality. Only after those gates should the page move into publishing and monitoring. The validation lane is important because it gives the team clear stop points before publishing. Intent match, no thin content and citation ready facts are not cosmetic. checks, they protect the usefulness and credibility of the page. The connected tools show how the loop continues after publication through the CMS, search console and analytics. The final takeaway is to optimize for humans first, then make the page machine readable, so search and AI systems can reliably interpret it.

**译文**：

在页面层面，SEO 不再只是将关键词放入标题，然后祈祷页面能够排名靠前。在人工智能搜索环境中，页面必须让爬虫、检索器和答案引擎都易于理解。视觉中心是一篇结构清晰的文章，因为优化真正就体现在这里。左侧，我们从三个输入开始：明确的意图、有用的结构和可信的证据。这些是帮助页面满足真实用户需求的基础。右侧，同一页面被搜索结果和人工智能答案系统所解读。核心理念很简单：当页面围绕用户意图组织，并以证据为支撑、以清晰的方式撰写时，它就更容易被爬虫抓取、更容易被识别、也更容易被总结。对于本讲座而言，可以将页面 SEO 视为连接人类实用性与机器可读性的桥梁。一个优质的页面通常遵循可重复的质量流程：它始于搜索意图，因为页面必须匹配搜索者想要完成的目标。接下来，内容简报将这一意图转化为计划，包括目标受众、关键问题、角度、来源和成功指标。然后是页面结构。 标题层级、逻辑结构和可扫描的格式有助于读者和检索系统理解页面内容。第4步添加了证据和实体，这能增强页面的可信度，而实体则帮助搜索系统将内容与已知主题、人物、产品、地点和属性关联起来。最后，页面会依据质量检查清单发布。其成果不仅是一个能够排名的页面，更是一个能够被引用到AI答案中、被包含在摘要里或被选为有用来源的页面。实际启示在于：应将页面设计为结构化的答案，而不仅仅是关键词目标。内容质量由多种协同作用的信号共同构建。原创见解至关重要，因为无论是AI搜索系统还是传统搜索引擎，都需要能够超越已有信息、提供附加价值的内容。主题覆盖范围有助于页面回答核心问题及相关后续问题。实体清晰性同样重要，因为明确的人物、概念、产品和属性名称能更轻松地与知识图谱或检索索引建立关联。当主题随时间变化时，内容的时效性尤为关键，尤其是在涉及工具、定价、政策和行业实践等领域。 当页面说明了信息背后的作者身份及其资质时，作者信誉能够增强用户的信心。提升用户体验则能确保页面易于访问，包括采用简短的段落、有用的标题以及清晰的团队格式——这些信号中没有任何一个能单独发挥作用。它们共同协作，打造出一个既对人类用户友好，又能被搜索引擎、E.I.A. 及相关系统理解的页面。现在，我们将“质量”转化为具体的页面元素。标题标签（title tag）在搜索结果中设定了页面的承诺，而 H1 标题则在用户进入页面后确认了其主要主题。H2 标题将页面划分为有意义的子主题，使内容更易于浏览和分块获取。内部链接（Internal link）将当前页面与相关页面连接起来，帮助用户和爬虫更好地理解整个主题集群。模式化标记（schema markup）可添加结构化数据，以明确页面类型、作者、日期、常见问题解答（FAQ）内容及其他重要信息。FAQ 版块只有在回答真实问题而非填充无意义内容时才真正有用。右侧的文件列表提醒我们，页面质量还需通过技术文件和结构化格式来支撑，其中包括 Markdown 内容、模式化数据、robots.txt 以及 sitemap.xml。 实践中的教训是，内容和结构应当协同工作。当一篇文章的含义在页面上被清晰地编码时，它才会更具价值。在一个真实的……团队中，页面 SEO 应被视为一种质量审核流程，而非最后时刻的清单。草稿的撰写始于研究、清晰的提纲、参考资料和内部审核。随后，SEO 审核会检查意图匹配度、关键词覆盖情况、标题、元信息以及内部链接。证据核查则通过审查可信度、事实准确性、数据时效性以及引文或链接的质量，来防止页面出现薄弱的论断。只有在通过这些关卡之后，页面才能进入发布和监控阶段。验证环节之所以重要，是因为它为团队在发布前提供了明确的停驻点。意图匹配、无薄弱内容以及可供引用的事实，这些都不是表面功夫的检查项，它们保护着页面的实用性和可信度。相关的工具展示了页面发布后如何通过 CMS、搜索控制台和分析工具持续优化。最终的启示是：首先为人类用户进行优化，然后再让页面具备机器可读性，这样搜索引擎和 AI 系统才能可靠地理解它。

## P18 18 - Website Architecture and Internal Linking

**原文**：

Website architecture is one of the quiet foundations of AISU. When your site is organized around clear hubs, supporting pages and evidence-rich content, crawlers and AI systems have a much easier time understanding what the business does, where it operates and which pages support each answer. The visual starts with a simple hierarchy. Home at the top then service location and knowledge hubs, then deeper pages like service pages, city pages, FAQs and case studies. The important idea is not just having more pages, it is connecting those pages in a way that communicates meaning. page gives local context, FAQs answer common prompts, case studies provide proof together the structure creates stronger answer candidates. For AI search, internal links should help users, crawlers and answer engines move from broad intent to specific supported evidence without confusion. because they turn isolated content into discoverable paths, start with a page that is indexable and worth finding. Then add a navigation link or hub link so the page is not hidden from the main site journey. From there, connect related pages using descriptive anchors that explain what the next page is about. That context helps crawlers discover the relationship between pages, but it also helps AI systems retrieve stronger evidence when a user asks a natural language question. Notice the progression in the workflow, publish, connect, relate, crawl, retrieve, a page that has no meaningful incoming links may technically exist, but it is much harder to treat as important. In practical terms, every important page should be reachable through navigation, hub pages, breadcrumbs, contextual links, or related content blocks. The goal is to make the best answer path obvious before an AI system has to infer it. A strong internal linking system has several repeatable building blocks. Topic hubs or... organized authority by grouping pages around a central theme, such as technical SEO, local SEO, or product optimization. Breadcams show hierarchy which helps both visitors and crawlers understand where a page sits in the site. Contextual links are linked placed inside meaningful content, not just in a footer or sidebar, so they connect. ideas while the reader is already engaged. Descriptive anchors matter because the anchor text should explain the destination, not just say click here. Related content blocks help continue the journey from one useful page to the next. HTML and XML site maps support discovery, especially on larger sites. The practical tip. takeaway is to design links intentionally, do not link only because a page exists. Link when the destination helps complete the user's question, expands the topic, proves a claim, or moves the visitor closer to action. Orphan pages are valuable pages that are not properly connected from the rest of the site. They may appear in an x. similar side map or they may be published in the CMS, but if there are no internal links pointing to them, they are weak signals for discovery and importance. The audit flow here starts with the side map. Then identifies pages with no internal links, weak anchor text or too many clicks between the homepage and the destination. those pages move into an audit queue, the fix is not simply adding random links. The best correction is to link from a relevant hub, use anchor text that explains the intent and reduce crawl depth when the page is strategically important. For example, a revenue driving service page should not be buried 5 clicks deep. The core rule is simple. Every valuable page needs a crawlable path and that path should make sense to a human reader first. At enterprise scale, website architecture cannot depend on memory or individual editors making perfect choices every time. It needs a governance loop. Begin with a content inventory. So, the team knows which URL templates, assets and sex. already exist. Then define architecture rules for hierarchy, link placement, anchor quality, metadata, and approval standards. CMS templates help enforce those rules by default. So each new page is more likely to include the right navigation, related links, and structured content blocks. Publishing review. adds human judgment and quality control. AI citation testing checks whether important content is retrievable and supported by relevant evidence. Search Console, GA4, Bing Webmaster Tools and LogFiles provide feedback about discovery, engagement and crawl behavior. The shipping checklist on the right is the operating principle. Link should be relevant, anchors should be clear, important pages should be reachable and evidence should be easy to retrieve.

**译文**：

网站架构是 AISU 的隐性基础之一。当您的网站围绕清晰的中心节点、支持页面和内容丰富的资料进行组织时，爬虫和 AI 系统就能更轻松地理解企业的业务内容、运营范围以及哪些页面为每个答案提供支持。该结构首先采用简单的层级：首页位于顶部，其次是服务地点和知识库中心，再往下则是服务页面、城市页面、常见问题解答和案例研究等更深入的页面。关键在于，不仅仅是增加页面数量，而是以能够传达意义的方式将这些页面连接起来。每个页面提供本地化语境，常见问题解答回应常见的查询，案例研究则提供证明；整体结构共同构建出更有力的答案候选。对于 AI 搜索而言，内部链接应帮助用户、爬虫和答案引擎从广泛意图顺畅过渡到具体的支撑证据，而不会产生混淆。由于内部链接能将孤立内容转化为可发现的路径，因此应首先确保页面可被索引且值得被找到。然后添加导航链接或中心节点链接，使该页面不会在网站主流程中被隐藏。接着，使用描述性锚文本连接相关页面，明确说明下一页的内容主题。 这种上下文有助于爬虫发现页面之间的关系，也有助于 AI 系统在用户提出自然语言问题时检索到更有力的证据。注意工作流程的递进关系：发布、连接、关联、抓取、检索。一个没有任何有意义入站链接的页面，虽然技术上可能存在，但要被视为重要页面则要困难得多。从实践角度来看，每个重要页面都应可通过导航、中心页面、面包屑导航、上下文链接或相关内容区块访问到。目标是在 AI 系统需要推断之前，就让最佳答案的路径变得显而易见。一个强大的内部链接系统包含多个可重复使用的构建模块。主题中心……通过将页面围绕某一核心主题（例如技术 SEO、本地 SEO 或产品优化）进行分组来组织权威性。面包屑导航可显示层级结构，帮助访问者和爬虫理解某页面在网站中的位置。上下文链接是指嵌入在有意义内容中的链接，而不仅仅是放在页脚或侧边栏中，因此它们可以在读者已经投入阅读时连接不同观点。描述性锚文本很重要，因为锚文本应当说明目标页面的内容，而不仅仅是“点击此处”。 相关的区块内容有助于用户从当前有用的页面继续前往下一个页面。HTML 和 XML 站点地图有助于提升页面的可发现性，尤其是在大型网站上。实用建议是：要有意识地设计链接，不要仅仅因为某个页面存在就进行链接。只有当目标页面能够帮助用户解答问题、扩展主题、验证观点，或促使访问者更接近采取行动时，才应建立链接。孤立页面是指那些有价值但未能从网站其他部分正确连接的页面。它们可能出现在类似侧边栏的地图中，也可能已发布在内容管理系统（CMS）中，但如果没有内部链接指向它们，就会削弱其被发现和体现重要性的信号。此处的审核流程首先从侧边栏地图开始，然后识别那些没有内部链接、锚文本薄弱，或从主页到目标页面点击次数过多的页面。这些页面会被放入审核队列中。解决方法并非简单地添加随机链接。最佳修正方式是从相关的中心页面建立链接，使用能说明意图的锚文本，并在页面具有重要战略意义时减少其抓取深度。例如，一个能带来收入的页面不应被埋藏在需要点击 5 次才能到达的位置。核心规则很简单。 每个有价值的页面都需要一个可爬取的路径，而该路径首先必须对人类读者来说合乎逻辑。在企业级规模下，网站架构不能依赖记忆，也不能指望每位编辑每次都做出完美选择。它需要一个治理闭环。首先从内容盘点开始。这样，团队就能清楚哪些 URL 模板、资产和性别已经存在。然后定义架构规则，涵盖层级结构、链接位置、锚文本质量、元数据以及审批标准。CMS 模板有助于默认强制执行这些规则。因此，每个新页面更有可能包含正确的导航、相关链接和结构化内容块。发布审核则加入了人工判断和质量控制。AI 引用测试可检查重要内容是否可检索，并由相关证据支持。Search Console、GA4、Bing Webmaster Tools 和 LogFiles 提供了有关发现、互动和爬取行为的反馈。右侧的上线检查清单就是其运作原则。链接应相关，锚文本应清晰，重要页面应可达，证据应易于检索。

## P19 19 - Topical Authority and E-E-A-T

**原文**：

Topical authority is the idea that a site becomes more useful when it covers a subject with depth, structure and consistent evidence. For AISEO, that matters because answer engines do not only look for one isolated page, they often need a clear source that explains a topic supports the explanation and connects related concepts in a way. that is easy to retrieve. E E 80 gives us a quality lens for that work, experience, expertise, authority, and trust. It is not a magic checklist or a single button we can turn on. It is a practical standard for building pages that feel credible to humans and easier for search systems to understand. The key takeaway is simple. Authority grows when topic coverage, proof and site structure reinforce each other. When those three align, your content has a stronger chance of being selected, cited and trusted. First, collect the questions, buyers, readers or users actually ask. Then, group those questions into topic clusters, so each cluster has a clear purpose. After that create evidence pages that answer the important questions with examples, data, screenshots, comparisons, definitions or expert review, internal links then connect the clusters so a crawler, a user or an AI retrieval system can move from the broad topic to supporting details. The final goal is not just more content. the goal is retrievable clarity. When an AI answer engine needs a source it should be able to find a page that answers the question directly and nearby supporting pages that prove the answer is trustworthy. This is how topic maps become sources that can support answer inclusion. EET becomes easier to apply when we treat each signal. as a content design requirement. Experience asks whether the page reflects first hand use or original observation. Expertise asks whether the explanation is accurate, complete, and reviewed by someone qualified for the topic. Authority asks whether the brand, author, or page is recognized by relevant sources, citations, link. or industry references. Trust anchors the system. If ownership is unclear, sources are missing, claims are exaggerated, or the page feels unsafe, the other signals lose value. For practical SEO work, avoid simply adding an author bio and assuming the job is done. The page itself should demonstrate these signals. Use example. transfer in sourcing, updated information, clear authorship and honest limitations. The strongest content does not only say it is credible, it makes credibility visible. In an enterprise environment, authority needs a repeatable workflow. Start with the content brief that defines the topic, search intent, audience use case, internal links and sixth. Next, collect evidence from subject matter experts, customer conversations, product data, tests or real implementation experience. Then the draft moves through editorial review for accuracy, clarity, balance and internal linking. schema and metadata help make the page easier to interpret, but they should describe real content, not invent credit. A quality gate is important because weak pages should not move straight to publishing. If the draft is not useful, accurate and trustworthy, it should go back for improvement. Once published, the work continues through analytics, search console and AI search testing. Authority is not an accident, it is a controlled process. A practical audit asks one direct question, can AI search trust this site enough to use it as a source? Start with coverage, look for missing clusters, often pages, and pages that answer the wrong intent. Then examine proof. Pages should include examples, data, expert review, and citations where the topic requires. the port. Finally, check trust. Users and systems should be able to identify the author, the organization, the policy context, and the reason a claim should be believed. The bottom flow is the optimization loop. Week pages become candidates for evidence upgrades and better evidence creates stronger retrieval candidates. This is also a useful way. to prioritize work. Do not rewrite every page at once. Focus first on high value topics when missing proof or poor linking prevents your best content from becoming a trusted answer source.

**译文**：

主题权威性是指，当一个网站以深度、结构和一致性的证据来覆盖某个主题时，该网站会变得更加有用。对于 AISEO 而言，这一点至关重要，因为答案引擎不仅仅寻找一个孤立的页面，它们往往需要一个清晰的来源来解释某个主题、支持该解释，并以易于检索的方式连接相关概念。E-E-A-T 为我们提供了评估这一工作的质量视角——即经验、专业知识、权威性和可信度。它并非一份神奇的检查清单，也不是我们可以一键开启的开关。它是一种实用标准，用于构建既让人类感到可信、又让搜索引擎系统更容易理解的页面。关键结论很简单：当主题覆盖范围、证据和网站结构相互强化时，权威性便会增长。当这三者协调一致时，你的内容被选中、被引用和受信任的可能性就会更大。首先，收集买家、读者或用户实际提出的问题。然后，将这些问题分组为主题集群，使每个集群都有明确的目的。 之后，创建用于解答关键问题的证据页面，内容可包括示例、数据、截图、对比、定义或专家评测，并添加内部链接。随后，将这些页面连接成网络，使网络爬虫、用户或AI检索系统能够从宏观主题逐步深入到支持性细节。最终目标并非单纯增加内容，而是实现“可检索的清晰度”。当AI问答引擎需要引用来源时，应能直接找到解答问题的页面，并快速定位到附近的支持性页面，以证明答案的可靠性。正是通过这种方式，主题图谱才能转化为可支撑答案收录的来源。当我们将每一个评估信号都视为内容设计的一项要求时，EET（经验、专业、权威、信任）的应用就会变得更加容易。 “经验”要求页面内容是否反映了第一手使用体验或原创观察；“专业”要求解释是否准确、完整，并由具备相关资质的专家审核；“权威”要求品牌、作者或页面是否被相关来源、引用、链接或行业参考资料所认可；而“信任”则是整个体系的基石。如果所有权不明、来源缺失、主张夸大，或页面给人带来不安全感，那么其他评估信号的价值都将随之削弱。 对于实际的 SEO 工作而言，避免仅仅添加一篇作者简介后就认为任务完成。页面本身应当体现这些信号。例如，在信息引用、更新内容、明确作者身份以及坦诚说明局限性等方面做到位。最优质的内容不仅声称自己可信，更会让这种可信度显而易见。在企业环境中，权威性需要通过可重复的工作流程来实现。首先从内容简报开始，明确主题、搜索意图、受众使用场景、内部链接以及目标。接下来，从领域专家、客户对话、产品数据、测试或真实实施经验中收集证据。然后，草稿将进入编辑审核环节，以确保准确性、清晰度、平衡性以及内部链接的合理性。schema 和元数据有助于让页面更易于被理解，但它们应描述真实内容，而非虚构可信度。设置质量关卡非常重要，因为不合格的页面不应直接进入发布阶段。如果草稿不具备实用性、准确性和可信度，就应退回修改。发布之后，工作仍将持续，包括通过分析工具、搜索控制台以及 AI 搜索测试进行跟进。权威性并非偶然形成，而是一个受控的过程。 一次实用的审计只问一个直接的问题：AI 搜索是否足够信任这个网站，能将其用作信息来源？首先从覆盖范围开始，查找缺失的聚类（通常是页面），以及那些未能匹配用户意图的页面。然后检查证据。页面应包含示例、数据、专家审核，以及在主题需要的情况下提供引用。最后，检查可信度。用户和系统应能识别作者、组织、政策背景，以及为何该说法值得信赖。底部的流程是优化循环。表现不佳的页面会成为证据升级的候选对象，而更优质的证据则会生成更可靠的检索候选内容。这也是一种有效的工作优先级排序方式。不要一次性重写所有页面。首先关注高价值主题，尤其是在缺乏证据或链接质量差导致优质内容无法成为可信答案来源时。

## P20 20 - AI-Generated Content, Quality and Spam Risks

**原文**：

AI-generated content is not automatically helpful and it is not automatically spam. The difference comes from purpose, evidence and review. On the left, the useful path represents content that starts with a real user need adds evidence, contributes an original angle and passes through human review. On the right, the risky path represents the same AI draft used in the wrong way, thin pages, scale pages, and unverified claims. That distinction matters for AI SEO because search engines and answer engines are trying to surface content that deserves trust. AI can speed up research, outlining, and drafting, but it cannot replace editorial judgment. The practical mindset is simple. Do not ask can AI generate this page? Ask does this page deserve to exist for a real user? That question keeps AI content aligned with quality instead of volume. A CIF AI content process works like a controlled publishing pipeline, not a one-click content machine. The workflow begins with a prompt and brief because the model needs a clear audience, intent, constraints and source expectations. The draft is only an intermediate artifact. Before it becomes a page, it should pass through a quality gate where the team checks whether the claims are supportable, whether the page is sufficiently original, and whether the search intent is actually served. checking and human editing, turn a usable draft into a publishable asset. The warning branch is important, when drafts bypass review, risk moves directly into production. That is how teams accidentally create thin pages, duplicated ideas or claims that nobody can defend. Monitoring after publishing is useful, but it is not a... substitute for pre-publish review. The strongest control happens before the URL goes live. The most common AI content problems are usually not caused by the model alone. They come from weak publishing incentives. Scale pages happen when teams generate many near duplicate pages for every keyword variation. Doorway content tries to capture traffic without offering a genuinely distinct answer. Scraped re-rides repackage someone else's work without adding meaningful value. No source evidence means the article sounds confident but cannot show where the information came from. Keyword stuffing is the old SEO mistake in a new wrapper where the language is optimized for a bit. instead of clarity. Fake authority is especially dangerous because AI can make unsupported expertise sound polished. For AI SEO, the goal is not to avoid automation entirely. The goal is to avoid using automation to mass produce low differentiation pages faster than your review process can verify them. What deprived teams need guardrails because AI content quality is a system problem, not only a right or problem. Editorial policy defines what good content means for the brand. Voice, claim standards, citation expectations, compliance boundaries and approval rules. The source log keeps track of where facts came from, what changed and who. approve those changes. SME review brings subject matter expertise into the process, especially for pages that affect money, safety, legal decisions or technical implementation. Analytics feedback closes the loop after publishing by showing whether the page is meeting user needs or creating issues. The pass, revise and holds state's turn quality judgment into an operational decision. This is the practical value of governance. It makes AI output accountable. Instead of asking individual creators to remember every rule, the workflow itself guides content toward publishable standards. A simple checklist prevents AI content review from becoming vague or circum- objective. Start with user intent. The page should answer a real question or help someone make a real decision. Then verify the facts, especially statistics, product details, legal claims, medical statements or anything time sensitive. Sources should be traceable, so reviewers can understand where claims came from. Original value is the next filter. A page should add experience, examples, comparison, analysis, data, or a clearer explanation than the generic version of the topic. Brand-tone matters because AI output often sounds polished but generic. Finally, every important page needs an update owner because quality can decay over time. The publish revise over time. whole decision should come from these checks. That is how teams scale with AI while still protecting trust, reputation and long-term search performance.

**译文**：

AI生成的内容并不自动具有帮助性，也不自动属于垃圾内容。其区别源于目的、依据和审核。在左侧，有用的路径代表了以真实用户需求为起点、提供依据、贡献独特视角并经过人工审核的内容。在右侧，高风险的路径则代表了以错误方式使用的相同AI草稿、内容稀薄的页面、规模化生产的页面以及未经核实的说法。这种区别对于AI SEO至关重要，因为搜索引擎和答案引擎都试图呈现值得信赖的内容。AI可以加速研究、提纲撰写和草稿生成，但无法取代编辑判断。实践中的思路很简单：不要问“AI能否生成这个页面？”，而要问“这个页面是否值得为真实用户存在？”。这个问题能让AI内容专注于质量而非数量。一个CIF AI内容流程更像是一个受控的发布流水线，而非一键式内容生成机器。工作流程始于提示和简报，因为模型需要明确的受众、意图、限制以及来源期望。草稿仅是一个中间产物。 在成为页面之前，它应该通过一个质量关卡，团队会在此检查其主张是否有据可依、页面是否具有足够的原创性，以及是否真正满足了搜索意图。通过检查和人工编辑，可以将一份可用的草稿转变为可发布的资产。预警分支非常重要，当草稿绕过审核时，风险就会直接进入生产环境。团队就是这样意外地创建了内容稀薄的页面、重复的想法或无人能够辩护的主张。发布后的监控很有用，但它并不能……替代发布前的审核。最强的控制措施发生在 URL 上线之前。最常见的 AI 内容问题通常并非由模型单独造成，而是源于薄弱的发布激励机制。当团队针对每一个关键词变体生成大量近乎重复的页面时，就会出现规模化页面。门户型内容试图获取流量，却没有提供真正独特的答案。抄袭内容则重新包装他人的作品，却没有增加有意义的价值。没有来源证据意味着文章听起来充满自信，却无法显示信息来自何处。 关键词堆砌是一种旧有的 SEO 错误，如今换了一种新形式，语言被优化得略显刻意，而非追求清晰。虚假权威尤其危险，因为 AI 能让缺乏依据的专业知识听起来条理分明。对于 AI SEO 而言，目标并非完全避免自动化，而是避免利用自动化大规模生产差异化极低的页面，其速度超过审核流程的验证能力。缺乏监管的团队需要设置防护机制，因为 AI 内容质量是一个系统性问题，而不仅仅是某个人的失误或问题。编辑政策界定了优质内容对品牌的定义，包括语气、主张标准、引用规范、合规边界以及审批规则。来源日志会记录事实出处、内容变更内容以及批准变更的人员。SME（领域专家）审核将专业见解引入流程，尤其适用于涉及金钱、安全、法律决策或技术实施的页面。分析反馈则在发布后形成闭环，通过数据表明页面是否满足了用户需求或引发了问题。“通过、修订和保留”的状态将质量判断转化为运营决策。这正是治理机制的实际价值所在。 它能让 AI 的输出结果具备可问责性。与其要求每位创作者记住所有规则，不如让工作流程本身引导内容达到可发布标准。一份简单的清单能防止 AI 内容审核变得模糊或流于形式。首先从用户意图开始。页面应当回答一个真实的问题，或帮助用户做出真实的决策。接着验证事实，尤其是统计数据、产品详情、法律声明、医疗陈述或任何与时效相关的内容。来源必须可追溯，以便审核人员能够了解相关说法的依据。原创价值是下一道筛选标准。页面应当提供独特的体验、案例、对比、分析、数据，或比该主题的通用版本更清晰的解释。品牌语调也很重要，因为 AI 生成的内容往往听起来精致却千篇一律。最后，每个重要页面都需要指定一位更新负责人，因为内容质量会随着时间的推移而下降。发布后应持续修订。整个决策过程都应基于这些检查。这正是团队在规模化使用 AI 的同时，仍能维护信任、声誉和长期搜索表现的方式。

## P21 21 - Workshop Complete a Traditional SEO Foundation Audit

**原文**：

Before we optimize for chat GPT Gemini perplexity or Google AI experiences, we need to know whether the site has a clean SEO foundation. A traditional SEO audit answers four basic questions. Can search systems discover the pages? Can the important pages be indexed? Is the content clear and useful enough to understand? and other pages connected with sensible internal links. The command center view is a simple way to think about this workshop. We are not trying to chase every possible ranking factor. We are building a baseline that tells us whether the site is healthy enough to support AI search optimization. Notice how the audit connects probability index flexibility on page quality and internal linking to AI readiness. If the foundation is weak, AI answer engines may have fewer reliable signals to retrieve, site, summarise or recommend the site. The goal is to leave with a practical fixed list, not just a technical report. The workshop follows a simple sequence. Next crawl the site so we have a complete URL inventory and can see status codes, redirects, canonical tags, titles, headings and link patterns. Next export the signals into a working format such as a spreadsheet because the audit has to become sortable and actionable. When we diagnose the issues instead of treating every warning as equally important. A missing title on an important service page is not the same as a minor issue on an old tag page. After diagnosis, we prioritize fixes by impact and effort. The output is the SEO foundation backlog, a focused list of changes that should be fixed before we move deeper into AI search optimization. the audit practical. It turns raw tool data into decisions and it gives you a repeatable process you can use for any website. Now let's break down what we are checking. Technical Access comes first, robots.txt, XML site maps, HTTP status codes, and whether important areas of the site are reachable. Canonical signals help us understand which version of a page should be treated as the primary version, especially when redirects duplicate URLs or parameter URLs exist. Page quality focuses on titles, headings, unique content, and whether the page satisfies a real searching site structure looks at navigation and internal links. because search engines and AI systems need paths to discover relationships between pages. Performance basics include speed and mobile usability, these are not the entire audit, but they are important signals for user experience and technical health. The key is to check these areas separately, then connect the findings. A strong foundation is not... one metric, it is a set of signals working together. A common mistake is ending the audit with screenshots and tool exports. In this workshop, the audit has to become an execution sheet. On the left, we collect evidence from crawl tools, Google Search Console, PageSpeed Insights and Site Search. Those sources help us see the problem. from different angles. The center table turns each finding into a row with a URL issue, evidence, priority, owner, and status. That structure matters because a real SEO audit needs accountability. Someone must know what to fix, why it matters and how progress will be tracked. On the right, the status flow keeps the work honest. Open, fixing and validated, validated is important. A fix is not complete just because someone changed a page. We need to recall, recheck evidence and confirm the issue is actually resolved. The final output of the workshop is an AI ready SEO baseline. Before the audit, the site may have messy URLs, crawl errors, no. index problems, redirect chains, duplicate content, thin pages, missing metadata, or old pages that send confusing signals. The middle of the visual turns those problems into a controlled process, confirm access, fix critical issues, and recheck the evidence. After the audit, the goal is not perfection. The clean validated foundation were important pages of crawlable, indexable, useful and connected. That gives traditional search engines and AI answer engines a better starting point. The practical takeaway is simple, AI visibility does not replace SEO fundamentals. It depends on them. Once the foundation is stable, lead. to work on topical authority, citations, structured content, and AI answer optimization becomes much more effective.

**译文**：

在我们针对 ChatGPT、Gemini、Perplexity 或 Google AI 体验进行优化之前，需要先了解网站是否具备扎实的 SEO 基础。传统的 SEO 审计可以回答四个基本问题：搜索引擎能否发现这些页面？重要页面能否被索引？内容是否清晰且足够有用？其他页面之间是否通过合理的内部链接相互关联？“指挥中心视图”是理解本次研讨会的简单方式。我们并非试图追逐每一个可能的排名因素，而是建立一个基准，判断网站是否足够健康，能够支持 AI 搜索优化。请注意，此次审计如何将概率、索引、灵活性、页面质量和内部链接与 AI 就绪状态联系起来。如果基础薄弱，AI 问答引擎可能缺乏可靠的信号来检索、总结或推荐该网站。我们的目标是带着一份实用的待修复清单离开，而不仅仅是一份技术报告。本次研讨会遵循一个简单的流程：接下来对网站进行抓取，以便我们获得完整的 URL 清单，并查看状态码、重定向、规范标签、标题、标题标签和链接模式。 接下来，将信号导出为可操作格式（例如电子表格），因为审核结果必须能够排序并采取行动。在诊断问题时，我们不应将所有警告视为同等重要。重要服务页面缺少标题，与旧标签页面上的小问题并不相同。诊断结束后，我们根据影响力和工作量对修复进行优先级排序。最终输出是 SEO 基础待办事项列表，这是一份需要优先修复的变更清单，之后我们才会深入进行 AI 搜索优化。该审核方法非常实用，它能将原始工具数据转化为决策，并为您提供一个适用于任何网站的标准化流程。现在，我们来分解具体检查内容。首先是技术可访问性：robots.txt、XML 站点地图、HTTP 状态码，以及网站重要区域是否可访问。规范信号有助于我们确定哪个页面版本应被视为主要版本，尤其是在存在重定向或参数化 URL 的情况下。页面质量则关注标题、标题标签、独特内容，以及页面是否满足实际需求。网站结构则侧重于导航和内部链接。 因为搜索引擎和 AI 系统需要路径来发现页面之间的关系。性能基础包括速度和移动设备可用性，这些并非完整的审计内容，但它们是用户体验和技术健康状况的重要信号。关键在于分别检查这些领域，然后将各项发现联系起来。一个坚实的基础并非……单一指标，而是一组协同工作的信号。一个常见的错误是在审计结束时仅以截图和工具导出作为成果。在本次工作坊中，审计必须转化为一张执行表。左侧，我们收集来自抓取工具、Google Search Console、PageSpeed Insights 和 Site Search 的证据。这些来源能帮助我们从不同角度看清问题。中间的表格将每个发现转化为一行，包含 URL、问题、证据、优先级、负责人和状态。这种结构很重要，因为真正的 SEO 审计需要明确责任。必须有人清楚要修复什么、为什么重要，以及如何跟踪进展。右侧的状态流能确保工作真实可信。状态分为“开放”、“修复中”和“已验证”，其中“已验证”至关重要。仅仅有人修改了页面，并不意味着修复工作已经完成。 我们需要回顾、重新检查证据，并确认问题确实已解决。本次工作坊的最终成果是一份可供 AI 使用的 SEO 基准。在审计之前，网站可能存在杂乱的 URL、爬行错误、无法索引的问题、重定向链、重复内容、内容稀少的页面、缺失元数据，或发送混淆信号的旧页面。视觉流程的中段将这些问题分析转化为可控的过程，确认访问权限，修复关键问题，并重新检查证据。审计之后的目标并非追求完美，而是建立一个经过验证的清晰基础，确保重要页面具备可爬行、可索引、有用且相互关联的特性。这为传统搜索引擎和 AI 答案引擎提供了更良好的起点。实际启示很简单：AI 可见性并不能取代 SEO 基础，它依赖于这些基础。一旦基础稳固，再着手提升主题权威性、引用、结构化内容以及 AI 答案优化，就会更加有效。

## P22 22 - Keywords vs AI Search Prompts

**原文**：

When we talk about keywords versus AI search prompts, the biggest shift is not just the wording, the shift is in how search systems interpret what the user really wants. Traditional search usually begins with short keyword strings, such as a product name, a problem, or a modifier. The engine matches those terms, ranks pages, and leaps. the user to open results and piece together the answer. AI search behaves differently. It works much better when the user expresses intent in natural language, including the goal, the context, and the desired outcome. That is why the visual compares a simple keyword query with a fuller AI prompt and then shows a synthesized answer. For AI SEO, this matters because content can no longer be optimized only around isolated terms. We need to optimize for answerability, context, and usefulness. In other words, visibility increasingly comes from helping AI systems understand the question deeply enough to generate the right response. Now let's compare the two searching. experiences as a process. In the traditional flow, the user submits a keyword query, the search engine retrieves pages and snippets, the user scans those pages and eventually extracts the answer manually. That process still works, but it depends heavily on the user doing the comparison and synthesis. In the AI search flow, the user starts with the natural language prompt. The system passes intent and context, retrieve, supporting evidence, reasons across that evidence and returns a more structured answer. This is an important mindset change for SEO teams. The job is no longer only to rank for clicks. The job is also to make your information easy for AI systems to interpret, retrieve and symbol. That means clear entities, strong structure, trustworthy evidence, and concise explanations become even more valuable. Search intent still matters, but AI search adds context, reasoning, and answer shaping on top of the classic retrieval model. A useful way to teach this topic is to show how a key. becomes a better prompt. A short keyword like best CRM for start-ups gives us a topic, but not in up direction. A stronger prompt adds the goal such as comparing options, the audience such as SaaS start-ups, constraints such as a budget cap and the output shape such as a rank table with pros and cons. The same logic applies to product recommendations, content ideation and campaign planning. Notice how each example turns a vague phrase into something more actionable and precise. That is exactly how users interact with chat, GPT, Gemini, Perplexity, and Google AI experiences. For AI SEO, the lesson is private. Study the keywords people already use, then expand them into prompt patterns that reveal deeper needs. Those patterns help you create pages, FAQs, comparisons, and supporting content that better match how AI assisted search journeys now unfold. Once we move beyond draw keywords, we can think in terms of a prompt. blueprint, the center of this slide shows the formula, goal plus context plus constraints plus output. Around that code, the supporting components make the prompt stronger. The task defines what the user wants to achieve. Context explains who the answer is for and what background matters. Constraints narrow the answer using factors. like budget, region, timeline or preferences. Evidence reminds us that many AI search tasks need trustworthy sources or citations. Output format matters because users often want a table, a step list, a comparison or another clear structure. And notice the reminder at the bottom, keywords still matter. They often carry the core entity, modifier or problem statement. In AI SEO, the opportunity is not to abandon keywords. It is to preserve the search intent inside a richer prompt structure, so content can answer with more precision, clarity and relevance. To close, this workflow shows how teams can operationalize AI SEO instead. of treating it as a one-off tactic. The process starts with discovering search intent from keywords, questions, and jobs to be done. Next, the team builds prompt patterns that reflect the major query types such as explanation, comparison, and recommendation. Then those prompts are tested across AI search environments like chatGPT, Google AI, Gemini and Perplexity to see how the brand appears and what sources are used. From there, the team improves answerability through better structures, stronger entities, fresher supporting content and clearer citations. Finally, outcomes are measured using mentions, visibility, traffic and conversions. The lower row highlights the... operating system behind the workflow, a prompt library, content quality checks, human editorial review, and brand rules, that combination is what makes AI SEO scalable. The code takeaway is simple, success comes from optimizing not just for rankings, but for high quality answers that AI systems can confidently retrieve. understand and present.

**译文**：

当我们讨论关键词与 AI 搜索提示词时，最大的转变并不仅仅在于措辞，而在于搜索系统如何解读用户的真实意图。传统搜索通常以简短的关键词字符串开始，例如产品名称、问题或修饰词。搜索引擎会匹配这些术语，对页面进行排序，然后让用户打开结果并自行拼凑答案。AI 搜索的运作方式则不同。当用户用自然语言表达自己的意图时——包括目标、背景信息和期望结果——它的效果要好得多。这就是为什么该图示将简单的关键词查询与更完整的 AI 提示词进行比较，然后展示一个综合生成的答案。对于 AI SEO 而言，这一点至关重要，因为内容不能再仅仅围绕孤立的关键词进行优化。我们需要针对可回答性、上下文和实用性进行优化。换句话说，能否获得曝光，越来越取决于我们是否能帮助 AI 系统深入理解问题，从而生成正确的回应。现在，让我们将这两种搜索体验作为一个过程进行比较。 在传统流程中，用户提交关键词查询，搜索引擎检索页面和摘要，用户浏览这些页面，并最终手动提取答案。该流程仍然有效，但严重依赖用户自行进行比较和综合。在 AI 搜索流程中，用户以自然语言提示开始。系统传递意图和上下文，检索支持性证据，基于这些证据进行推理，并返回结构更清晰的答案。这对 SEO 团队而言是一种重要的思维转变。其任务不再仅仅是为了点击率而获得排名，还要让自己的信息易于 AI 系统理解、检索和提取。这意味着清晰的实体、强大的结构、可靠的证据以及简洁的解释变得更有价值。搜索意图仍然重要，但 AI 搜索在传统的检索模型基础上，增加了上下文、推理和答案塑造。一种有效的教学方式是展示一个关键词如何演变为更优质的提示。像“适合初创公司的最佳 CRM”这样的简短关键词虽然提供了主题，但并未指明方向。 更强大的提示会加入目标（例如比较不同选项）、受众（例如 SaaS 初创公司）、限制条件（例如预算上限）以及输出格式（例如包含优缺点的排名表格）。同样的逻辑也适用于产品推荐、内容构思和活动策划。请注意，每个示例都是如何将一个模糊的表述转化为更具可操作性和精确性的内容。这正是用户与聊天机器人、GPT、Gemini、Perplexity 以及 Google AI 等产品交互的方式。对于 AI SEO 而言，其启示是：深入研究人们实际使用的关键词，然后将其扩展为能够揭示深层需求的提示模式。这些模式能帮助你创建更符合当前 AI 辅助搜索流程的页面、常见问题解答、对比内容以及辅助资料。一旦我们不再局限于关键词本身，就可以开始以“提示蓝图”的思维进行规划。本幻灯片中央展示了其核心公式：目标 + 背景 + 限制条件 + 输出。围绕这一核心，辅助组件能够进一步增强提示的效果。“任务”定义了用户希望达成的目标；“背景”说明了答案的适用对象以及相关背景信息；“限制条件”则通过预算、地区、时间线或偏好等因素缩小答案范围。 有证据表明，许多 AI 搜索任务需要可信的来源或引用。输出格式很重要，因为用户通常希望看到表格、分步列表、对比表或其他清晰的结构。同时请注意底部的提醒：关键词仍然至关重要。它们通常承载着核心实体、修饰语或问题陈述。在 AI SEO 中，关键不是放弃关键词，而是在更丰富的提示结构中保留搜索意图，从而使内容能够以更精准、清晰且相关的方式作答。最后，这一工作流程展示了团队如何真正将 AI SEO 付诸实践，而非仅将其视为一次性策略。该流程首先通过关键词、问题和待完成任务来挖掘搜索意图。接下来，团队构建能够反映主要查询类型（如解释、对比和推荐）的提示模板。然后，这些提示会在 chatGPT、Google AI、Gemini 和 Perplexity 等 AI 搜索环境中进行测试，以观察品牌的呈现方式以及所使用的来源。在此基础上，团队通过更优的结构、更明确的实体、更新鲜的支撑内容和更清晰的引用来提升内容的可答性。 最后，结果通过提及次数、可见性、流量和转化来衡量。下面一行突出了工作流背后的……操作系统、提示词库、内容质量检查、人工编辑审核和品牌规则，正是这种组合使得 AI SEO 具备可扩展性。从代码中得出的结论很简单：成功不仅来自于优化排名，更来自于优化高质量的答案，使 AI 系统能够自信地检索到这些答案。理解并呈现出来。

## P23 23 - Informational, Comparison and Recommendation Prompts

**原文**：

We begin with the big idea behind this lecture. AI search prompts are not all trying to achieve the same outcome. Some prompts are informational, where the user wants to learn or understand something, some are comparison prompts, where the goal is to evaluate options side by side. Others are recommendation prompts, where the user wants a best it answer and guidance on what to do next. The visual starts with user need because that is the correct starting point. Before writing any prompt, ask what the user is actually trying to accomplish. If the need is understanding, the prompt should invite explanation. If the need is evaluation, the prompt should frame trade-offs. If the need is a decision. the prom should request a recommendation with reasoning. This matters in AISU because better prompt matching usually leads to more useful, more structured and more satisfying answers from AI search systems. Let's look more closely at informational prompts. These are the prompts we use when the main goal is learning. The process on the slide shows 5 pranks. first start with the topic so the model knows what you want to learn. Second, add context such as the audience industry or use case. Third, set scope so the answer is not too broad or too shallow. Fourth, ask for a format because AI answers improve when you specify whether you want bullets, a summary. checklist or another structure. Finally, review the response for clarity, completeness and accuracy. Notice the prompt formula at the bottom. It turns a vague question into a much better instruction by combining topic, audience, format, key points and constraints. For AI SEO, this is extremely useful when researching definition. processes and unfamiliar concepts before creating content or optimization plans. Comperson prompts are designed for evaluation. Instead of asking AI for one loose opinion, we ask it to judge multiple options against the same criteria. That is why the center of the slide uses a comparison grid. A strong comparison. identifies the options clearly defines shared evaluation criteria and keeps the level of detail consistent across every option. When those pieces are present, the answer becomes much more trustworthy and much easier to act on. Also, notice the example prompt at the bottom. It asks AI to compare tools using specific damage. such as speed, citations, prompt flexibility, and ease of use. That level of structure helps AI produce trade-offs rather than vague praise. In AI SEO work, comparison prompts are helpful when choosing platforms, evaluating content workflows, comparing prompt strategies, or deciding which AI search tool fits a bit. particular job best. Recommendation prompts go one step further than comparison prompts. Here the user is not just exploring options. The user wants AI to suggest the best fit for a specific context. The slide shows the ingredients that make these prompts effective. Start by describing the situation. Then list the options being controlled. considered, define the decision criteria, specify the preferred output and ask for reasoning. On the right, you can see the kind of answer we want back, a best option, an explanation of why it fits and a clear next step. That structure is especially powerful in AISU because recommendations often depend on constraints such as budget, team size, time. online or skill level. When you include context and success criteria, the AI has a much better chance of giving a realistic answer instead of a generic one. The final step is choosing the right prompt type before you begin. This slide brings the lecture together by connecting user goal to output type. If the goal is to learn user information. if the goal is to evaluate user comparison prompt. If the goal is to decide user recommendation prompt, that simple classification step can dramatically improve output quality because it aligns the prompt with the answer style the AI system should produce. The workflow at the bottom is a useful habit. Identify intent. choose the prompt type, add constraints and format, and then check output quality. For AI SEO practitioners, this is not just a writing trick. It is a repeatable method for generating clearer research, better decision support, and more actionable answers across tools like chatGPT, Gemini, Perplexity and Google AI Search experiences. Thanks for watching, and I'll see you in the next video, and I'll see you in the next video.

**译文**：

我们从本讲座的核心思想开始。AI 搜索提示并非都旨在实现相同的结果。有些提示是信息型的，用户希望学习或理解某些内容；有些是比較型的，目标是逐项评估不同选项；还有一些是推荐型的，用户希望得到一个最佳答案以及下一步该做什么的指导。图示从用户需求开始，因为这才是正确的起点。在编写任何提示之前，请先问自己：用户实际上想完成什么？如果需求是理解，提示应引导模型进行解释；如果需求是评估，提示应呈现权衡因素；如果需求是做出决策，提示则应请求附带推理过程的推荐。这一点在 AISU 中很重要，因为更匹配的提示通常能带来更有用、结构更清晰、更令人满意的 AI 搜索答案。让我们更仔细地看看信息型提示。当我们主要目标是学习时，就会使用这类提示。幻灯片上的流程展示了 5 个步骤：首先确定主题，这样模型就知道您想了解什么；其次补充背景信息，例如目标受众、行业或使用场景。 第三，设定范围，使答案既不过于宽泛，也不过于浅显。第四，要求指定格式，因为当明确说明需要项目符号、摘要、检查清单或其他结构时，AI 的回答质量会更高。最后，检查回复的清晰度、完整性和准确性。请注意底部的提示公式。它将主题、受众、格式、关键点和约束条件结合起来，把一个模糊的问题转化为更清晰的指令。对于 AI 搜索引擎优化（SEO）而言，在创建内容或优化计划前，研究定义、流程和陌生概念时，这种方法尤其有用。对比提示则专为评估而设计。我们不要求 AI 给出一个模糊的观点，而是让它根据相同的标准对多个选项进行评判。这就是幻灯片中心部分使用对比表格的原因。一个有效的对比，应清晰列出各选项，明确共同的评估标准，并在所有选项中保持一致的细节层次。具备这些要素后，答案的可信度和可操作性都会大幅提升。另外，请注意底部的示例提示。它要求 AI 使用特定的标准来比较工具。 例如速度、引用、提示灵活性以及易用性。这种结构有助于 AI 生成权衡分析，而非空洞的赞美。在 AI SEO 工作中，当选择平台、评估内容工作流程、比较提示策略或决定哪款 AI 搜索工具最适合某项特定任务时，比较提示会很有帮助。推荐提示则比比较提示更进一步。此时用户不仅仅是探索选项，而是希望 AI 针对特定情境推荐最佳方案。该幻灯片展示了使这些提示有效的要素。首先描述情境，然后列出正在考虑的选项，明确决策标准，指定期望的输出，并要求提供推理过程。在右侧，您可以看到我们希望得到的答案类型：一个最佳选项、其适用性解释以及一个明确的下一步。这种结构在 AISU 中尤其强大，因为推荐通常取决于预算、团队规模、时间、在线资源或技能水平等限制因素。当您提供了情境和成功标准后，AI 更有可能给出切合实际的回答，而非泛泛而谈。 在开始之前，最后一步是选择正确的提示类型。本幻灯片通过将用户目标与输出类型关联起来，将整个讲座内容串联起来。如果目标是了解用户信息，就选择信息类提示；如果目标是评估用户，就选择比较类提示；如果目标是决定用户推荐，就选择推荐类提示。这个简单的分类步骤可以显著提高输出质量，因为它使提示与 AI 系统应生成的答案风格相匹配。底部的流程是一个很有用的习惯：明确意图，选择提示类型，添加约束和格式，然后检查输出质量。对于 AI SEO 从业者来说，这不仅仅是一种写作技巧，而是一种可重复使用的方法，可用于在 ChatGPT、Gemini、Perplexity 和 Google AI Search 等工具中生成更清晰的研究结果、更优质的决策支持以及更具可行性的答案。感谢您的观看，我们下个视频再见，我们下个视频再见。

## P24 24 - Conversational Search and Follow-Up Questions

**原文**：

Conversational search changes the SEO problem from winning one query to supporting a sequence of questions. In a traditional search session, the user often rewrites a query manually and scans several results. In an AI answer engine, the user can ask an initial question, receive a synthesized answer, and then refine the task without starting over. The context from the first turn matters because it shapes what the system thinks the user wants next. For AI SEO, this means content should not only answer the first broad question, it should also make the next likely question easier to answer. A page that defines the topic, explain straight-offs, gives examples and supports comparison. can remain useful across several turns. The key idea is simple, optimize for the conversation path not just the first search phrase. Follow-up questions work because the answer engine carries context forward. The user asks the system interprets intent, retrieves information, creates an answer, and then receives a refinement. That refinement may change the scope, add a constraint, request a comparison or ask for evidence. The important part is that the second question is really isolated. It depends on what was already discussed. From an optimization perspective, this creates a practical checklist. Your content needs clear sections that can be retrieved independently. but those sections should still connect back to the larger topic. Definitions help interpretation, structured headings help retrieval, specific examples help the answer. Follow up friendly content reduces ambiguity because the AI system can reuse prior context and combine it with a more precise next request. questions fall into a small set of useful patterns. A clarification follow-up narrows the scope such as asking which option fits a small business instead of every business. A comparison follow-up asks the system to rank alternatives or explain trade-offs. A narrowing follow-up asks filters like budget, location, industry, or... tolerance. An expansion follow-up asks for more context examples or H cases. A verification follow-up checks sources, dates, credentials or supporting evidence. Finally, an action follow-up asks for the next step, checklist, template or recommendation. When creating content for AI search, these patterns are valuable before. because they reveal the missing pieces around the topic. If your page only answers the first question, another source may be selected when the user asks the second one. A strong answer how is designed to serve the next question before the user asks it. Start with definition, so the system can understand the topic cleanly. Add comparisons so it can explain difference. without inventing trade-offs, include decision criteria, so recommendations can be tied to real requirements, add proof points such as examples, process details, author expertise, and source references where appropriate. The validation lane on the right matters because conversation and search often pushes users toward trust questions. Ask whether the answer is current, whether it is backed by sources, or whether a recommendation applies to their situation. Content that is structured around definitions, comparisons, criteria, and evidence gives AI systems more reliable material to retrieve inside during follow-up terms. To apply this in practice, treat follow-up questions as a search signal. Start with prompt mining. Collect real questions from search logs, site search, chat transcripts, sales calls, support tickets and AI platform testing. Then group those questions into intent clusters so you can see patterns instead of isolated prompts. Next look for content gaps. A gap may be a missing definition. week comparison, absent criteria or an unanswered recommendation scenario. After that, create answer assets, improve sections, FAQs, comparison tables, checklists, examples and source backed explanations. Finally, measure whether your content appears in AI answers, citations and follow-up paths. The work? flow should repeat, test prompts, improve pages, and retest. The takeaway is that conversational SEO is an ongoing feedback loop, not a one-time keyword task.

**译文**：

对话式搜索将 SEO 问题从赢得单次查询转变为支持一连串的问题。在传统搜索会话中，用户通常需要手动改写查询并浏览多个结果。在 AI 答案引擎中，用户可以提出初始问题，获得一个综合性的答案，然后在不重新开始的情况下优化任务。第一次交互的上下文至关重要，因为它决定了系统认为用户接下来想要什么。对于 AI SEO 而言，这意味着内容不仅要能回答最初那个宽泛的问题，还应让下一个可能的问题更容易被解答。一个能够定义主题、直接解释、提供示例并支持比较的页面，可以在多次交互中持续保持价值。其核心理念很简单：为整个对话路径优化，而不仅仅是首次搜索的关键词。后续问题之所以有效，是因为答案引擎会将上下文延续下去。用户提出问题，系统解读意图、检索信息、生成答案，然后收到用户的进一步说明。这种进一步说明可能会改变范围、增加限制、要求进行比较或索要证据。关键在于，第二个问题并非真正孤立的。 这取决于之前已经讨论过什么。从优化的角度来看，这会形成一个实用的检查清单。你的内容需要有可以独立检索的清晰章节，但这些章节仍需与更大的主题保持关联。定义有助于理解，结构化的标题有助于检索，具体的示例有助于生成答案。友好的后续内容可以减少歧义，因为 AI 系统可以复用之前的上下文，并将其与更精确的下一个请求相结合。问题可以归为少数几种有用的模式。澄清类后续问题会缩小范围，例如询问哪种选项适合小型企业，而不是所有企业。比较类后续问题要求系统对备选方案进行排序或解释权衡关系。收窄类后续问题会使用预算、地点、行业或……容忍度等筛选条件。扩展类后续问题会要求提供更多上下文示例或更多案例。验证类后续问题会检查来源、日期、资质或支持证据。最后是行动类后续问题，它会询问下一步、检查清单、模板或建议。在为 AI 搜索创建内容时，这些模式在前期阶段很有价值。 因为它们揭示了围绕该主题的缺失部分。如果您的页面只回答了第一个问题，当用户提出第二个问题时，系统可能会选择另一个来源。一个强有力的回答旨在在用户提问之前就为下一个问题做好准备。从定义开始，这样系统就能清晰地理解主题。加入比较，以便解释差异。不要凭空创造权衡取舍，而是加入决策标准，这样推荐内容才能与实际需求挂钩。加入证明点，例如示例、流程细节、作者专业背景以及在适当时引用的来源。右侧的验证部分很重要，因为对话和搜索常常会引导用户产生信任方面的疑问。例如：答案是否最新？是否有来源支持？某项推荐是否适用于他们的具体情况？以定义、比较、标准和证据为结构组织内容，可以为 AI 系统在后续检索中提供更可靠的素材。要实际应用这一点，可将后续问题视为一种搜索信号。从提示挖掘开始。 从搜索日志、站内搜索、聊天记录、销售通话、支持工单和 AI 平台测试中收集真实问题。然后将这些问题按意图聚类，以便发现模式，而非孤立的提示。接着寻找内容缺口。缺口可能表现为缺失的定义、周对比、缺失的标准或未解答的建议场景。之后，创建答案资产，完善相关版块、常见问题解答、对比表、核对清单、示例以及有来源支撑的解释。最后，衡量你的内容是否出现在 AI 答案、引用和后续路径中。该工作流程应持续迭代：测试提示、优化页面、再测试。关键在于，对话式 SEO 是一个持续的反馈循环，而非一次性关键词任务。

## P25 25 - Mapping Customer Problems and Use Cases

**原文**：

Before we build content for AI search, we have to change the starting question. Traditional SEO often begins with a keyword list. AI search optimization begins one level earlier. With the customer problem that causes the search, a person does not ask chart GPT, Gemini, Perplexity, or Google AI mode a random prompt in isolation. They usually have a pain point, a decision to make or a task they want to complete. The visual connects that chain. The problem creates the search moment. The search moment becomes an AI answer. And the answer should lead to what a useful business action. That is why mapping problems comes before writing prompts. When we understand the underlying problem. We can create content that is more likely to be selected, cited, summarized and trusted by answer engines. Now let's turn the idea into a repeatable workflow. Start by collecting problem signals from real sources such as sales questions, support tickets, on-site search logs, reviews, customer interviews, and forum discussions. Next group those signals by intent. The goal is not to collect every possible phrase. The goal is to find repeatable patterns in what customers are trying to solve. After that, translate the intent clusters into prompt variants because AI search users ask in full questions, comparisons, and scenarios. Then match each prompt family to the best. answer format, a short explanation, checklist, comparison table, troubleshooting guide or recommendation page. Finally, prioritize the content actions, the refine loop matters because search behavior changes as customers learn more. A good AI SEO workflow keeps coming back to the evidence instead of relying on one time keyword research. Long, a useful customer problem map is more than a persona worksheet. Each use case needs enough context to guide the content strategy. Audience tells us who has the problem. Problem defines what is blocking them. Objection identifies the risk doubt or reason they may hesitate. Trigger tells us when the search happens, which often changes the wording. of the prompt. Decision explains what choice the customer is trying to make. Proof tells us what evidence the answer needs to be credible. These six parts help us avoid vague content like best tools or complete guide without a clear purpose. For AI search, this is especially important because answer engines compress information. If our page does not clearly address the problem, decision and proof requirements, it may be less useful as a sighted source. In an enterprise environment, customer problem mapping should become an editorial operating system, not just a brainstorming exercise. The inputs come from places where customers already reveal their needs, CRM notes. Support tickets, sales calls and search logs. Those inputs feed a use case board that turns raw signals into content opportunities. Compare use cases become comparison pages. Recommend use cases become decision support content. Trouble should use cases become how two guides or fixed workflows. Decide use cases need proof. risk reduction and sauce backed answers. The governance layer is important to review protects accuracy, accuracy protects trust, and freshness keeps the content useful as products, prices, policies, or search behavior change. The practical takeaway is simple. Treat use cases as living assets that connect customer evidence to content Once the use cases are listed, the next mistake is trying to build everything at once. Prioritization keeps the strategy focused. The vertical axis represents customer pain. The horizontal axis represents business value. The highest priority is the top-right quadrant, where customers have a real problem and solving it also supports business. goals. Those are the use cases to build first. High pain, but lower value topics may still be worth supporting later, because they improve customer experience and trust. Low pain and low value ideas should be monitored rather than rushed. Low pain, but high value ideas may be deferred until the evidence becomes stronger. This matrix also... health teams have better conversations. Instead of arguing over isolated keywords, they can rank use cases based on customer need, commercial impact, and the answer quality required to win AI search visibility.

**译文**：

在为我们的人工智能搜索构建内容之前，我们必须先改变最初的问题。传统的搜索引擎优化（SEO）通常从关键词列表开始。而人工智能搜索优化则要早一个层级。它始于引发搜索的客户问题——人们不会孤立地向 Chart GPT、Gemini、Perplexity 或 Google AI 模式提出一个随机的提示。他们通常有痛点、需要做出的决定，或想要完成的任务。图表将这一连串环节连接起来：问题催生了搜索时刻，搜索时刻转化为人工智能的答案，而答案最终应引导至有效的商业行动。这就是为什么在撰写提示之前，必须先绘制问题图谱。当我们理解了根本问题，就能创作出更有可能被答案引擎选中、引用、总结并信赖的内容。现在，让我们将这一理念转化为可重复执行的流程。首先，从真实来源收集问题信号，例如销售咨询、支持工单、站内搜索日志、评论、客户访谈以及论坛讨论。接下来，根据意图对这些信号进行分组。目标不是收集所有可能的措辞，而是找出客户试图解决的重复性模式。 之后，需要将意图聚类转化为提示词变体，因为 AI 搜索用户会以完整问题、对比和场景的形式提问。然后，将每个提示词家族匹配到最合适的答案格式，例如简短解释、清单、对比表、故障排除指南或推荐页面。最后，要优先安排内容操作，因为随着客户了解更多信息，搜索行为会发生变化，所以优化循环至关重要。一个优秀的 AI SEO 工作流程会持续回归到实际证据，而不是依赖一次性的关键词研究。一份详尽且有用的客户问题地图，远不止是一份用户画像工作表。每个用例都需要足够的上下文来指导内容策略。受众告诉我们谁有这个问题。问题界定了是什么在阻碍他们。异议识别了他们可能犹豫的风险、疑虑或原因。触发因素告诉我们搜索发生的时间，而这往往会改变提示词的措辞。决策解释了客户试图做出什么选择。证明则说明了答案需要哪些证据才能具备可信度。这六个部分能帮助我们避免制作出“最佳工具”或“完整指南”这类缺乏明确目的的模糊内容。 对于 AI 搜索而言，这一点尤为重要，因为答案引擎会对信息进行压缩。如果我们的页面未能清晰地阐述问题、决策和证明要求，那么它作为可见来源的实用性可能会降低。在企业环境中，客户问题映射应成为一种编辑操作系统，而不仅仅是一次头脑风暴。输入信息来自客户已经暴露其需求的各类渠道，例如 CRM 笔记、支持工单、销售通话和搜索日志。这些输入会汇入一个用例看板，将原始信号转化为内容机会。对比用例可转化为对比页面，推荐用例可转化为决策支持内容，故障用例可转化为操作指南或标准化流程，而决策用例则需要提供证明、风险降低依据以及有来源支撑的答案。治理层至关重要，它负责审核内容的准确性，而准确性能建立信任，内容的时效性则能确保其在产品、价格、政策或搜索行为发生变化时依然有用。其实际启示很简单：将用例视为连接客户证据与内容的动态资产。一旦用例被列出，接下来常见的错误就是试图一次性构建所有内容。 优先级划分能让战略保持聚焦。纵轴代表客户痛点，横轴代表业务价值。最高优先级位于右上角象限——客户存在真实问题，而解决该问题同样能支持业务目标。这些应作为首先构建的用例。高痛点但低价值的议题可能仍值得后续支持，因为它们能改善客户体验和信任度。低痛点且低价值的想法则应持续观察，而非仓促推进。低痛点但高价值的想法则可暂缓实施，直至相关证据更加充分。该矩阵还能……让健康团队开展更高效的讨论。他们无需再围绕孤立的关键词争论，而是可以根据客户需求的紧迫性、商业影响力以及赢得 AI 搜索可见性所需的答案质量，对各类用例进行排序。

## P26 26 - Building a Prompt Universe

**原文**：

A prompt universe is the complete set of ways a customer might ask an AI system about a problem, a product category, a comparison or a recommendation before they ever reach your website. In traditional SEO, we often start with one keyword and a search volume number. In AI search, that single keyword expands into many natural language. Notice how the brand and topic sit in the center while the surrounding categories represent the major directions of customer might go. Someone may ask about a problem, a use case, a comparison, a recommendation, an objection or a specific context such as budget, industry, location or risk. The goal is not to change random prompts. The goal is to build a map that reflects real customer thinking. Once that map exists, you can decide what evidence, pages, FAQs, comparisons and examples your site needs in order to be useful when AI systems generate answers. The workflow begins with seed problems, not with keyword tools alone. A seed problem is the plain language pain point your customer would actually describe. From there you expand the question set. How they would ask chatGPT, Google AI, Gmail, or PURplexity for help? After expansion you cluster the prompts by intent so that similar needs sit together instead of creating a messy list. Then you map each cluster to a content. a guide, comparison page, product page, case study, checklist or FAQ. The final step is testing, prompts that look good in a spreadsheet may behave differently across AI search platforms. Some answers may cite competitors, some may miss your brand, and some may expose content gaps. This process gives you a repeat. way to move from raw customer language to a content plan that can actually be tested. A prompt universe becomes easier to manage when each prompt is treated as a record. The center of the visual is the prompt record, which holds fields such as prompt ID, intent cluster, target asset, owner and status, around it are the inputs that make record meaningful, customer role matters because of beginner, a technical buyer and an executive may ask very different questions. The problem statement keeps the prompt grounded in a real need. The use case tells you the situation where the question appears. Constraints capture budget, tools, geography, compliance or time limits. The decision stage shows whether the person is learning, comparing, validating or ready to act. Evidence needed tells the content team what proof the answer should contain. This structure prevents a prompt universe from becoming a random brainstorming document and turns it into an operational content asset. In the real team, the prompt universe. should live where it can be reviewed, owned and updated. The repository panel shows a practical pattern of prompt underscoreuniverse.csv file for the prompt records and evidence underscoremap.md file for supporting sources and documentation files such as codebase underscoremap.md clot.md and When AI assisted workflows are part of the operating model, the middle workflow is intentionally simple, review, approve, publish and measure. That means new prompts should not automatically become public content. They should be checked for customer relevance, evidence quality, brand fit and compliance. before the enter the publishing process. On the right, the prompt universe connects to the systems that make it useful, CMS, analytics, search console, MCP tools and LSP context. The takeaway is that prompts, evidence, owners and updates need to live together, otherwise the map becomes stale very quickly. The last part of the lecture is validation. A prompt universe is only valuable if you test it against the places where customers actually ask questions. The prompt set moves into different AI search experiences and the outputs are reviewed against practical criteria. Answer presents asks whether your brand product or content category appears at all. Citation quality asks whether the system references useful trustworthy sources rather than vague or outdated material. Entity accuracy checks whether the AI understands your company, product names, categories and differentiators correctly. Content gaps show where the answer is weak because your site does not provide enough evidence. Those gaps should not... in a report forever. They should become a content backlog with clear next actions. This closes the loop, prompts reveal demand, testing reveals visibility and the backlog turns those insights into better pages and stronger AI search coverage.

**译文**：

“提示词宇宙”是指客户在访问您网站之前，可能向 AI 系统提出有关问题、产品类别、比较或建议的所有方式的完整集合。在传统 SEO 中，我们通常从一个关键词和搜索量数据开始。而在 AI 搜索中，这个单一关键词会扩展为多种自然语言表达。请注意品牌和主题位于中心位置，而周围的类别则代表了客户可能采取的主要方向。有人可能会询问某个问题、使用场景、比较、建议、异议，或预算、行业、位置、风险等具体情境。目标不是随意更改提示词，而是构建一张反映真实客户思考过程的地图。一旦这张地图建立起来，您就可以确定网站需要哪些证据、页面、常见问题解答、比较内容和示例，以便在 AI 系统生成答案时提供有用信息。工作流程始于“种子问题”，而不仅仅是依赖关键词工具。“种子问题”是客户会用日常语言描述的实际痛点。在此基础上，您再扩展问题集：他们会如何向 ChatGPT、Google AI、Gmail 或 PURplexity 寻求帮助？ 在扩展之后，您需要按意图对提示进行聚类，使相似的需求归为一组，而不是形成一个杂乱无章的列表。然后，将每个聚类映射到一种内容类型：指南、对比页面、产品页面、案例研究、清单或常见问题解答。最后一步是测试：在电子表格中看起来效果良好的提示，在不同的 AI 搜索平台上的表现可能会有所不同。有些答案可能会引用竞争对手，有些可能会遗漏您的品牌，还有些可能会暴露内容缺口。这一流程为您提供了一种可重复的方法，可将原始客户语言转化为一个真正可以测试的内容计划。当每个提示都被视为一条记录时，提示宇宙就更容易管理了。视觉中心是提示记录，其中包含提示 ID、意图聚类、目标资产、负责人和状态等字段。围绕它的是使该记录具有意义的输入信息：客户角色很重要，因为新手、技术买家和高管提出的问题可能截然不同。问题陈述确保提示始终基于真实需求。用例则说明了该问题出现的情境。约束条件则涵盖了预算、工具、地域、合规性或时间限制。 决策阶段表明用户处于学习、比较、验证还是准备采取行动的阶段。所需证据则告知内容团队答案中应包含哪些证明。这种结构可防止“提示词宇宙”演变为随意的头脑风暴文档，而是将其转化为一个可操作的内容资产。在实际团队中，“提示词宇宙”应存储在一个便于审查、明确归属和随时更新的位置。存储库面板展示了一种实用模式：使用 `prompt_universe.csv` 文件存储提示词记录，使用 `evidence_map.md` 文件管理支持性来源和文档文件（如 `codebase_map.md`、`clot.md` 等）。当 AI 辅助工作流成为运营模式的一部分时，中间的工作流被刻意设计得简单明了：审查、批准、发布和衡量。这意味着新的提示词不应自动转为公开内容，在进入发布流程前，必须对其客户相关性、证据质量、品牌契合度和合规性进行检查。在右侧，“提示词宇宙”连接着使其发挥效用的各类系统，包括 CMS、分析工具、搜索控制台、MCP 工具和 LSP 上下文。 关键在于，提示、证据、所有者和更新必须协同存在，否则地图很快就会变得过时。讲座的最后一部分是验证。一个提示宇宙只有在针对客户实际提问的场景进行测试时才有价值。提示集会被应用到不同的 AI 搜索体验中，其输出结果会根据实际标准进行评估。“答案呈现”会检验你的品牌、产品或内容类别是否确实出现。“引用质量”会检验系统是否引用了有用且可信的来源，而非模糊或过时的材料。“实体准确性”则检查 AI 是否正确理解你的公司、产品名称、类别和差异化特点。“内容缺口”则显示答案薄弱的部分，因为你的网站未能提供足够的证据。这些缺口不应永远停留在报告中，而应转化为一个内容待办事项列表，并明确后续行动。这样就形成了闭环：提示揭示需求，测试揭示可见度，而内容待办事项则将这些洞察转化为更优质的页面和更强大的 AI 搜索覆盖能力。

## P27 27 - Prioritizing Prompts by Opportunity and Business Value

**原文**：

When you build a prompt universe, the list can become large very quickly. You may collect informational prompts, comparison prompts, recommendation prompts, and follow-up questions from many customer problems. The mistake is trying to optimize for everything at the same time. Prompt prioritization gives you a practical filter, we are not only asking goods. Someone asked this, we are asking whether winning that answer can improve visibility, trust or revenue. In this visual, the prompt universe enters a scoring lens. The lens combines opportunity signals such as search demand and answer gaps with business value signals such as relevance, authority, impact and revenue potential. The output is a ranked backlog, build first, test next or monitor. This matters because AI search optimization is an execution discipline. You need a clear reason to choose one prompt cluster before another. Otherwise the team spends effort on prompts that may look interesting but do not move the business. A useful prioritization work. flow starts before scoring. First, collect prompts from the places where customers already reveal their needs, search queries, sales calls, support tickets, AI results checks and competitor comparisons. Next, group those prompts by use case so you avoid treating every wording variation as a separate project. For examples, Several prompts may all represent the same customer problem, such as choosing a provider or comparing two solutions. After grouping score opportunity, that score reflects the gap between what AI systems currently answer and what a helpful well-supported answer should include, then score business value. That means estimating... Whether this use case can influence pipeline, trust, retention or strategic positioning. Only after both scores are visible, should you prioritize work? The final backlog is not just a list of keywords. It is a ranked set of prompt use cases with enough context for content, SEO, product, and sales teams to act on. The score. Adding model should stay simple enough that teams actually use it. On the opportunity side, look for prompts that appear often, expose a weak or incomplete AI answer, show competitive weakness and match content or proof you can realistically provide. A prompt with high opportunity usually means there is room to become a better sighted source answer. On the business value side, look at buyer intent, revenue influence, trust impact, and whether credible proof is available. Proof matters because AI systems prefer a useful grounded information over unsupported marketing claims. The formula at the bottom is intentionally practical. Priority equals opportunity multiple. by business value and confidence. Confidence keeps the team honest. If the data is weak, the score should not become a fall certainty. In practice, you can use a 1-5 scale for each signal, discuss disagreements quickly and focus on relative ranking rather than pretending the score is perfectly precise. Once prompts are scored, priority metrics helps translate numbers into decisions. The horizontal axis is opportunity and the vertical axis is business value. The upper right quadrant is built first. These are prompts where there is a strong chance to improve AI visibility and the business impact is meaningful. The upper left quadrant is validate first. These prompts may be valuable but opportunities uncertain so the right move is research, evidence gathering or a small test. The lower right quadrant is optimized efficiently. These may have opportunity but the business impact is lower so use lightweight updates rather than a major content project. The lower left quadrant is more. Keep an eye on trends, but do not spend heavy effort yet. The key lesson is that high opportunity alone is not enough. A prompt should earn priority because it also connects to real business outcomes. Prioritization only becomes valuable when it turns into a repeatable execution system. In a real team workflow, input sources. feed a prompt backlog. Search logs show what people look for, sales questions, reveal buying friction, support tickets, expose confusion, AI results check show where the brand appears where it is missing and where answers are incomplete. The backlog should include a score, an owner and a next action. That action might be creating... new content, adding proof, improving schema or updating FAQs. After workshops, measurement closes the loop. Track citations, ranking, traffic, conversions, and qualitative changes in AI answers. The goal is not to create a perfect spreadsheet. The goal is to make prompt research operational, a weekly backlog review can keep SEO content product in customer facing teams aligned around the prompts that deserve action now.

**译文**：

在构建提示词宇宙时，列表很快就会变得非常庞大。你可能会收集到来自众多客户问题的信息类提示词、对比类提示词、推荐类提示词以及后续问题。常见的错误是试图同时优化所有内容。提示词优先级排序为你提供了一个实用的筛选机制——我们不只是在询问“商品”本身。当有人提出某个问题时，我们真正要问的是：赢得这个问题的答案能否提升可见度、信任度或收入。在这一可视化流程中，提示词宇宙会进入一个评分透镜。该透镜将机会信号（如搜索需求和问题缺口）与业务价值信号（如相关性、权威性、影响力和收入潜力）相结合。最终输出的是一个排序后的待办列表：优先构建、后续测试或持续监控。这一点至关重要，因为 AI 搜索优化本质上是一种执行纪律。你需要一个明确的理由来决定优先选择某个提示词集群而非另一个。否则，团队可能会将精力耗费在一些看似有趣却无法推动业务发展的提示词上。一个有效的优先级排序工作流程，应从评分之前就开始。 首先，从客户已经表达需求的地方收集提示，例如搜索查询、销售通话、支持工单、AI 结果检查以及竞品对比。接下来，按使用场景对这些提示进行分组，避免将每一种措辞变体都视为独立项目。例如，多个提示可能都代表同一个客户问题，比如选择供应商或比较两种解决方案。分组后，评估机会得分——该分数反映当前 AI 系统提供的回答与一个有帮助且内容充实的回答之间存在的差距；然后评估商业价值得分，即估算该使用场景是否会影响销售线索、客户信任、留存率或战略定位。只有在两个得分都明确后，才能对工作优先级做出判断。最终的待办事项清单不仅仅是关键词的集合，而是一个经过排序的使用场景列表，其中包含足够的上下文信息，供内容、SEO、产品和销售团队采取行动。至于模型评分，应保持足够简单，以便团队能够真正加以使用。 从机会角度来看，请寻找那些频繁出现、能暴露出 AI 回答薄弱或不完整之处、展现竞争劣势，并且与你能够实际提供的内容或证据相匹配的提示。具有高机会的提示通常意味着存在成为更可靠信息来源答案的空间。从商业价值的角度来看，请考虑买家意图、收入影响力、信任影响，以及是否有可信的证据可用。证据之所以重要，是因为 AI 系统更倾向于实用且有据可依的信息，而非未经支持的营销主张。底部的公式是刻意设计得贴近实际的。优先级等于机会乘以商业价值和信心。信心能促使团队保持诚实。如果数据薄弱，分数就不应被视为绝对确定的依据。实践中，你可以为每个信号使用 1-5 的评分标准，快速讨论分歧，并专注于相对排名，而不是假装分数是绝对精确的。一旦提示被评分后，优先级指标有助于将数字转化为决策。横轴是机会，纵轴是商业价值。优先开发的是右上角象限。 这些提示词有很大可能提升 AI 可见度，且对业务的影响意义重大。左上象限应优先验证。这些提示词可能很有价值，但机会尚不确定，因此正确的做法是开展研究、收集证据或进行小范围测试。右下象限需高效优化。这些提示词可能存在机会，但业务影响较低，因此应采用轻量级更新，而非投入大型内容项目。左下象限则需持续关注。密切留意趋势，但暂不投入过多精力。关键启示在于：仅有高机会并不足够。一个提示词之所以应获得优先权，是因为它还与实际业务成果相关联。只有当优先级划分转化为可重复执行的系统时，其价值才能真正体现。在真实的团队工作流中，各类输入来源会为一个提示词待办池提供素材：搜索日志能显示用户关注点，销售咨询可揭示购买障碍，支持工单能暴露用户困惑，而 AI 结果检查则能发现品牌缺失的位置以及答案不完整之处。该待办池应包含评分、负责人及下一步行动。下一步行动可能是创建…… 新增内容、补充证据、优化方案或更新常见问题解答。研讨会结束后，通过衡量结果形成闭环。跟踪引用、排名、流量、转化以及 AI 回答在定性方面的变化。目标并非制作一份完美的电子表格，而是让提示词研究真正投入运营。通过每周审查待办事项，可确保面向客户的团队围绕当前亟需处理的提示词，在 SEO 内容产品上保持一致。
